username/password keeping role, member and tenant. Reset deliberately touches NO demo data —
it is tenant-scoped and shared between roles, so wiping it would take the tenant's other roles
with it; the confirmation dialog says so and the button is labelled "Reset Login".
`DemoReminderScheduler` runs daily at 08:00 America/Chicago, recomputing reminder dates from
each role's CURRENT end date every run, which is what makes "reminders recalculate when the
expiry changes" true by construction. Reminders go to the Service Admin (`demo.reminder.recipient`)
via the 3-arg `sendGenericEmail`, which carries no client id and is therefore not subject to the
demo send block — correct, since the recipient is the admin, not the tenant. `DemoRoleAdminController`
exposes list/add/reset/end-date/block plus the per-client delivery and reminder settings, and
backfills on read. UI is a new "Demo Role Access" panel in serviceadminhome.html, added
alongside the existing credentials panel rather than replacing it.

**2026-09-03: Demo/test access windows + send blocking (phases 1 and 3 of the demo-data work).**
Three new tables: `demo_role_access` (one access window per demo login, keyed by `signup.id`
because Reset reissues the username), `demo_client_settings` (per-tenant `allow_sms` /
`allow_email`, both defaulting to false, plus reminder intervals), `demo_reminder_log`
(includes the end date it was computed from, so moving an expiry re-arms its reminders).
`DemoAccessService` owns backfill, windows and the send guard. Enforcement is in
`LoginController` **after `orgClientId` is resolved** — `signup.client_id` holds the app_user id
for staff logins and the member ref for portal logins, so checking it directly catches only
church logins. `ensureWindow` creates a window on first login for demo accounts predating the
feature, because a missing row must never read as "no limit". Send blocking: `EmailService`
guards `sendOrgEmail` and the clientId-bearing `sendGenericEmail`; `SmsService.sendForClient`
is now used at 10 of 11 SMS call sites (the 11th is the Service Admin composer, deliberately
exempt). Still to do: Add Role / Reset Login / admin UI (phase 2) and the reminder scheduler
(phase 4).

**2026-09-03: Service Admin can send ad-hoc SMS and email.** New `Messaging` card on
`/serviceadminhome` with two tabs, backed by `ServiceAdminMessagingController` (guarded on the
same `role=ServiceAdmin` session attribute as the other service-admin APIs). It reuses the
existing Twilio account and mail configuration — nothing new is provisioned. `SmsService` gained
a From-override overload (`sendWithOutcome(to, body, from)`); `EmailService` gained
`sendComposed(to, bcc, subject, html, from, fromName)`, which unlike `doSend` **throws** on
failure so an admin pressing Send gets a real error instead of a stderr line. BCC goes through
`helper.setBcc`, so those addresses are SMTP envelope recipients only and appear in no header —
no BCC recipient can see the To list or the other BCC addresses. SMS validates every number to
E.164 up front and refuses the whole batch if any is unusable, rather than half-sending.
Verified with a full `mvnw compile` (669 sources, BUILD SUCCESS).

**2026-09-03: Event Image preview never displayed.** `.img-preview` carries `display:none` in the
stylesheet, but `onImageChange()` re-showed it with `p.style.display = ''`, which only clears the
*inline* declaration — the class rule kept winning, so no user ever saw the uploaded flyer before
saving. Now sets `'block'` explicitly. Copied into `target/classes/static/`. Found while recording
the product demo: the walkthrough waited 30s for a preview that could never appear.

**2026-08-31: `/events` delete left the card on screen until refresh.** `confirmDelete()` called `closeDeleteModal()` — which sets `deleteId = null` — *before* `allEvents.filter(ev => ev.id !== deleteId)`, so every row was compared against `null`, nothing was removed, and `filterCards()` re-rendered the identical list. The DELETE itself always succeeded, which is why a refresh showed the event gone; the success toast even named it, because `deleteName` was not cleared. Fix: capture `deleteId`/`deleteName` into locals at the top of `confirmDelete()` and use those for the request, the filter and the toast (comparison also `String()`-normalised so a string id from a future payload still matches). No backend change — `DELETE /api/events/{id}` was already returning 200. `events.html` copied into `target/classes/static/`; **restart/redeploy to serve it**.

**2026-08-30: Event reminder SMS under-delivery, plus three Event-page changes.**

**(4, the real one) Event-reminder SMS reached 2 of N recipients on 08/19 and nobody could tell why.** The honest finding is that the code could not answer the question: `ReminderSchedulerService.runEventReminders` recorded nothing per recipient, so a send that reached two people and one that reached forty produced identical evidence. Four defects each produce exactly that symptom, and all four are now fixed:
1. **No exception isolation anywhere on the path.** The recipient loop made three DB calls per iteration (`resolveMessageNoSettings` → `email_settings` lookup, `canSendSms` → plan + usage). Any throw abandoned every remaining recipient — *and* propagated out of `runHalfHourlyReminders`, cancelling meeting, holiday, one-time and prayer reminders for that tick. Compare `EventRegistrationReminderService.runRegistrationReminders`, which had per-event try/catch all along; this path never got it. Now isolated per sub-job, per rule, per event and per recipient, and the message body is built once per occurrence instead of once per person.
2. **Unusable phone numbers vanished silently.** `sendToPhoneMultiChannel` did `if (normalized == null) return;` with no log at any level. Now logged at WARN naming the registrant and recorded as SKIPPED.
3. **The provider's reason was thrown away.** `SmsService.send` collapsed everything to a boolean, so "replied STOP" (21610), "invalid number" (21211) and "A2P 10DLC campaign unregistered" (30034 — blocks nearly all US traffic and is the classic cause of this exact symptom) were indistinguishable. New `sendWithOutcome` returns a `SendOutcome(sent, errorCode, reason)` with those codes translated into something an admin can act on; `send()` still delegates so no caller changed.
4. **Nothing was recorded per recipient.** Every attempt now writes an `event_registration_reminder_log` row with SENT/SKIPPED/FAILED + reason, reusing that table with `message_type` of `SAME_DAY`/`BEFORE`/`AFTER` — distinct from its `REMINDER`/`INVITE`, and its dedupe is scoped by type, so the two features share the table without gating each other. Surfaced in the UI as a **Delivery** button on each Event Reminders row (the existing `GET /api/events/{id}/registration-reminder/logs` already returns them). Duplicate phones within one event now collapse to one message, and each run logs `"event 42 [SAME_DAY] SMS — 2 sent, 1 skipped, 37 failed (of 40 registrant(s))"`.

Also fixed while in here: `runHalfHourlyReminders` used `LocalDate.now()` (JVM default zone) while its cron is `zone="America/Chicago"` — the identical bug the file already documents and fixes for prayer reminders. On a UTC server every evening tick after 19:00 Chicago evaluated against tomorrow. Now `LocalDate.now(SCHED_ZONE)`. **Two things deliberately NOT changed, both need a decision:** (a) the reminder path does not check `sms_opt_in` at all while the registration-confirmation path does (`require-opt-in` defaults true) — adding it would *reduce* delivery further, and it is a TCPA/AUP question, not a bug fix; (b) `markSent` is still called *before* sending (deliberate anti-duplicate design) and its `@Transactional(REQUIRES_NEW)` is inert anyway, since it is a private method invoked from within the same class so no proxy applies. `docs/sms-reminder-diagnosis.sql` has read-only production queries for each candidate cause.

**(1) Event → Event Reminders.** After a *new* event saves, the Event page shows "Please set the reminder if you would like to set a reminder for …" with a link to `/eventReminders?eventId=<id>`. That page preselects the event and pre-ticks Same Day + Before 3 + After 1. Nothing is saved automatically — a page should not silently create a rule that texts a congregation. An event that already has a reminder opens it for editing rather than adding a second; an id not in the dropdown says so instead of leaving the field blank.

**(2) Event ID / Code.** Marked `optional` with a keyboard-reachable "?" explaining it is optional, manual-or-generated, always unique, and searchable. Uniqueness for manually typed codes is now enforced in `ChurchEventService.requireUniqueEventCode` (case-insensitive, trimmed, self-excluded on edit) throwing `DuplicateEventCodeException`; the controller returns `errorField: "eventCode"` so the page highlights that input. Previously only the DB unique index caught it, surfacing as an unattributed "Failed to save event".

**(3) Date** now carries the red asterisk for One Day events. The frontend validation was already correct and is unchanged.

Tests: `reminders/EventReminderDeliveryTest` (11) + `reminders/EventCodeUniquenessTest` (7). `./mvnw test` green at **280** (was 262). Mutation-checked: removing the per-recipient try/catch, the phone dedupe, the skip recording, the sub-job isolation, and the audit-write guard fails 5 of 11 — including one that reproduces the reported symptom exactly (recipients after a failure never attempted). Dropping the self-exclusion or the blank-code guard each fails the code tests. Front-end verified by 24 flow assertions against the real page source plus rendered screenshots. `event.html` and `eventReminders.html` copied into `target/classes/static/`; **redeploy required**, no schema migration needed.

**2026-08-29: Guess It group controls — editable duration, a readable join code, and multi-device sessions that stay independent.** Three changes to `/guessIt` Master Controls, all in `guessIt.html` + `GuessItGroupController`.

**(1) Duration is now a property of the GROUP, editable after creation, capped at 3 minutes.** New `guess_it_group.timer_secs` (default 60, `columnDefinition` supplied so ddl-auto can add it to a populated table) and `PUT /api/guess-it/admin/groups/{id}/timer`. Previously the only timer control was the global "Timer Per Clue" dropdown, which applied to the *currently active* game or was stashed in `localStorage` as a pending default for the next `start` — so once a group's games existed there was no way to change their duration. The endpoint writes the value on the group (rounds added later inherit it, via `createGroupGame`) and re-applies it to every game in the group that is not `completed`, including the live one; a completed game keeps the duration it was actually played with, because that is the record of how it ran. **Over-range values are clamped, not rejected** — a page left open from before the ceiling existed still works — and the response carries `clamped:true` so the dropdown can correct itself. `MAX_TIMER_SECS = 180` lives on `GuessItGroupController` and the standalone `/admin/games/{id}/timer` endpoint now shares it, so a game cannot end up outside the range the dropdowns can express. Both dropdowns are built from one JS array (`GG_DURATIONS`); a stored value that is not a preset is *added* to the list rather than snapping to a neighbour, so the control never lies about what is in force.

**(2) The group code was white text on the white admin panel — effectively invisible.** `ggAdminSelectGroup` rendered `Code <strong style="color:#fff">` inside `#adminBody`, whose background is white; the host could only read the code by selecting it. Replaced with `ggCodeChipHtml()`: a solid `#4a2233` block, 40px monospace, 10px letter-spacing, an Active/Ended pill and a Copy button (`navigator.clipboard` with a hidden-textarea fallback for plain-http). The "new group created" box now uses the same chip, so there is one definition instead of two divergent inline-styled ones.

**(3) Multi-device play: the participant token is now stored per group code.** It was one `localStorage` key, `gi_group_token`, for the whole browser — so joining a second group overwrote the first, and a stale token from a finished group resumed straight into it with no way back to the code screen except Leave. Now `gi_group_token:<CODE>` plus `gi_group_last`, with the legacy key migrated on first load (resolved against `/group/state` to learn which code it belonged to). Access goes through `ggStoreGet/Set/Del`, which fall back localStorage → sessionStorage → an in-memory map, because storage *throws* in Safari private browsing and under "block site data" — an uncaught throw there previously lost the session on every refresh. **The bigger fix is that a session is now only discarded when the server explicitly says the token is dead**: `resolve()` returns an `errorKey`, and only `expired` clears storage. Before, `ggPoll` called `ggLeave()` on *any* error — a withdrawn group or a momentary blip signed the participant out, and re-joining then collided with the name they themselves were still holding. Failed polls now back off (2s × failures, capped 15s) behind a "Reconnecting…" bar and keep the session. `join` and `verify` accept the token the device already holds and resume it, which makes joining idempotent under retries and refreshes. `playRowFor` catches the `DataIntegrityViolationException` from two simultaneous submissions by one device and re-reads the winning row instead of 500-ing. Nothing on the participant path reads or writes an HTTP session, which is what makes N devices on one code genuinely independent — verified by test rather than assumed.

Tests: `guessit/GuessItGroupDurationTest` (11) + `guessit/GuessItMultiDeviceTest` (9). `./mvnw test` green at **262** (was 242). All new tests were mutation-checked: removing the clamp, applying the duration to completed rounds, dropping the held-token resume, marking every resolve error as `expired`, and removing the race recovery each fail 7 of the 20. Front-end logic covered by a node/vm harness against the real page source (31 checks: per-code storage isolation, both storage fallbacks, dropdown construction, chip escaping). `migrate_production.sql` gained an idempotent `timer_secs` block plus a guarded trim of any stored value above 180. Updated `guessIt.html` copied into `target/classes/static/`; **production needs a redeploy and `migrate_production.sql` run first**.

**2026-08-27 (later): `/memberHome?tab=guessit` rendered its own JavaScript as visible page text.** The Guess It member-portal block (`ggm*`) had been pasted into the *middle of a JS template literal* — specifically inside `wsPrintAll()`'s `w.document.write(\`<!DOCTYPE html>…<body>${combined}` … `</body></html>\`);`, the string that builds the "print all worship schedules" popup. The HTML parser does not know or care that a `</script>` sits inside a JS string: **any literal `</script>` in script content ends the block**, so the closing tag at the end of the pasted section terminated the page's main `<script>` (opened ~line 2412) about 900 lines early. Everything after it was parsed as **body content**, which is why the screenshot shows raw source as text with real styled buttons ("Accept / Decline / Maybe", "You got it right!", "Game Over") interleaved — those are the HTML fragments inside the template literals, rendered as actual elements. The blast radius was far wider than the Guess It tab: `loadGuessit`, `stopGuessitPolling`, the `showTab` override and the tab-bar scroller all live below the break and **never defined at all**, so nothing after that point on the member portal worked. Fix: lifted the block out of the string (restoring `wsPrintAll`'s one-line `document.write`) and re-inserted it as its own top-level `<script>` after the main block closes; `ggm*` functions are only reached from inline `onclick` and its own `DOMContentLoaded`, so being later in the document is fine. Verified with `node --check` on all five inline blocks (all parse) and a stray-closing-tag scan across **all 129 static HTML files — memberHome.html was the only one affected**. Copied to `target/classes/static/` per the usual static-asset rule; production needs a redeploy.

**2026-08-27: `backup_config.retention_months` could not be added by ddl-auto — startup ALTER failed, Backup Configuration screen broken.** Starting the JAR against a database where `migrate_production.sql` had not been run produced `ERROR: column "retention_months" of relation "backup_config" contains null values` from Hibernate's `alter table if exists backup_config add column retention_months integer not null`. PostgreSQL rejects adding a NOT NULL column with **no default** to a table that already has rows, and `backup_config` always has exactly one (`BackupService.getConfig()` creates row id 1 on first use). Hibernate logs this as a **WARN and keeps starting** — the app comes up looking healthy while the column simply does not exist, so every later `backupConfigRepository.findById(1)` fails with `column bc1_0.retention_months does not exist`: the Service Admin backup screen and the daily retention purge are both dead, and nothing at startup says so. Fix: `@ColumnDefault("12")` on `BackupConfig.retentionMonths`, so the generated DDL is `... integer default 12 not null` and the ALTER both succeeds and backfills the existing row with the same value `migrate_production.sql` line 754 uses — the two paths now agree. Immediate unblock on an already-running database, no rebuild needed: `ALTER TABLE backup_config ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS retention_months INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 12;` (idempotent, and identical to what the migration script would have done). **General rule for this codebase**: a `@Column(nullable = false)` added to an entity whose table already holds rows MUST carry `@ColumnDefault`, or ddl-auto silently cannot apply it — mirroring it in `migrate_production.sql` only helps if somebody remembers to run that file before the deploy, which is exactly what did not happen here.

**2026-08-20 (later): event registration SMS never reached anyone — phone numbers went to Twilio exactly as typed.** `ChurchEventService.sendRegistrationSms` called `smsService.send(reg.getPhone().trim(), …)` with the raw contents of a free-text `<input type="tel">` that had no pattern and no validation. Twilio requires E.164 and returns [error 21211](https://www.twilio.com/docs/api/errors/21211) for anything else; `SmsService.send` catches its own exception, logs it and returns a boolean nobody checked, so the registration still succeeded, the confirmation email still went out, and the text silently never arrived. Checked against one event's real registration list: **seven of eight numbers were undeliverable** — only the single entry somebody happened to type with a leading `+` would have worked. New **`util/PhoneNumbers.toE164`** is now the one definition: strips punctuation, adds `+1` to a bare ten-digit number, handles the very common `1XXXXXXXXXX` (country code, no plus), passes explicit `+…` through so international numbers keep their own code, and validates the NANP dial plan (area code and exchange both start 2-9) so a pasted leading zero is caught here rather than failing out of sight at the provider. **It refuses rather than guesses** — a nine-digit entry like `270917242` returns null, because padding it would produce a real number belonging to a stranger who would then be texted somebody else's event details. `SmsService.send` normalises at the boundary too, so callers written later are covered by default. **Four independent copies of "normalize phone" existed and disagreed** — one returned `+1XXXXXXXXXX`, another bare ten digits, a third rejected `(913) 555-0100` on the punctuation; `SmsOptInController` and `WhatsAppSenderService` now delegate to the shared one. `EventRegistrationReminderService.normalizePhone` deliberately still returns the ten-digit national form, because `event_registration_reminder_log.recipient_norm` stores that shape and is matched by equality to decide whether a reminder already went out — rewriting it to E.164 would make every historical row stop matching and re-send reminders to people who already had one; `PhoneNumbers.nationalDigits` exists for exactly that case. **Two further gaps on the same path**: no opt-in check, despite `SmsService`'s own javadoc saying to always call `SmsOptInRepository.isOptedIn` first — now gated behind `sms.event-registration.require-opt-in` (default **true**; note the consequence, that a church with no opt-in records sends no registration texts at all, which is intended and logged per skip with the number and reason). The lookup uses the *normalised* number because `sms_opt_in.phone_number` is matched by exact string equality — matching on the raw value finds nothing, which reads as "not opted in" and would have suppressed every message for what looks like a consent decision and is actually a formatting bug. And the send now routes through `WhatsAppSenderService.sendSingleSms`, the existing choke-point that applies the plan's monthly SMS allowance and records usage; calling `SmsService.send` directly meant event texts never counted against a church's limit. Client side, `event-register.html` mirrors `toE164` in JS, validates the field on submit, reformats on blur so the visitor sees what will be stored, and posts the E.164 value — advisory only, since the server normalises again and anything can POST to the endpoint. `migrate_production.sql` backfills `event_registration.phone` and `sms_opt_in.phone_number` with the identical rules, is idempotent (rows already starting with `+` are excluded), and ends with a NOTICE plus a report query listing the rows it could not fix so they can be re-collected. The migration was **executed against a scratch PostgreSQL 16 with the real numbers as fixtures** and its output matched the Java and the JS case for case, including the four unfixable rows. `./mvnw test` green at 242 (24 new); the registration-SMS tests were mutation-checked — reverting the normalisation and the opt-in gate fails 8 of 11, and bypassing the quota choke-point fails 2 more. **Still unverified**: whether `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID`/`TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN`/`TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER` are set in the App Service configuration at all — if they are not, `isConfigured()` is false and none of this sends regardless.

**2026-08-20: Demo/test data gets a real subscription — plan choice + expiry.** Test/Demo Data → Load Test Data now asks which subscription plan the demo tenant should run on and when its access should end (30 days / 60 days / a custom date, default one year when nothing is chosen). **No new table and no migration**: `service_client` already *is* the per-tenant subscription record — `subscription_type` → `subscription_plan.plan_code` (via `SubscriptionService.toPlanCode`), plus `start_date`, `end_date`, `status` — so a demo load simply writes its own row like every other tenant, which is also why two demo loads cannot interfere with each other. Plans are read from `subscription_plan` and validated there (`TestDataService.resolvePlanCode`): inactive plans are refused, matching is case-insensitive but stores the database's canonical casing, an unknown code comes back with the list of real ones, and no plan name or id appears anywhere in the code. The dropdown is fed by the existing `GET /api/serviceadmin/subscription-plans` — the same endpoint the Subscription Plans screen uses — so it cannot offer a plan that does not exist. `POST /api/serviceadmin/test-data/load` takes an optional body (`planCode`, `expiresOn` or `expiresInDays`; all optional, so the old no-argument call still works) and refuses an expiry of today or earlier rather than handing back credentials that cannot sign in. New `PUT /api/serviceadmin/test-data/{clientId}/subscription` views/changes/extends a demo subscription by updating that tenant's existing row in place — there is nowhere for a duplicate subscription record to go — and is hard-guarded to the `DEMO-` prefix so it can never touch a paying church; extending past today also flips `status` back to `Active`, and the plan cache is cleared so the new limits apply at once instead of up to five minutes later. **Login enforcement closed a real gap**: staff and church logins were already gated by `countValidChurchLogin`/`countValidNonChurchLogin` (`sc.end_date > current_date`), but member and child portal logins have no `app_user` row, so that query returned 0 for them and the whole check was skipped — when a church's subscription lapsed the staff were locked out and the congregation was not. `LoginController` now resolves a portal login's owning church through `family_member` (including the existing member_ref-by-email fallback) and checks that subscription, and an expired staff/church login gets a dated "expired on …, contact your administrator to renew" message instead of the catch-all "Account access is restricted". The member check is deliberately **fail-open** — unresolvable linkage, missing `service_client` row, null end date or a lookup that throws all let the sign-in through, because a check that fails closed here would lock out every member whose `member_ref` was never backfilled, a far bigger outage than the one being fixed — and it looks at the date only, never `status`, which was never part of a member's sign-in. All of these refusals are recorded via `recordDenied` (audit only), not `recordFailure`, so a member with the correct password cannot throttle themselves out. `SubscriptionService.isExpired(endDate, today)` is now the single definition of "expired", shared by the login gate and the admin badge and written to match the SQL exactly: an end date of *today* is already expired, because the comparison is `>`, not `>=`. Service Admin shows plan, start date, expiry, days remaining and an Active/Expired badge per demo tenant, with an Edit/Extend dialog whose +30/+60 buttons count from **today** so extending an already-expired demo actually yields usable days. Verified: `./mvnw test` green, 218 tests (34 new — `DemoSubscriptionTest`, `SubscriptionExpiryLoginTest`), and the four tests covering the member gap were confirmed to fail against the unfixed code before being kept.

**2026-08-19: last-remaining-backup protection made PER TABLE, not per snapshot** — the retention rule agreed the previous day ("always keep the most recent backup even if older than retention") was implemented at snapshot-date granularity: keep the newest snapshot whole, drop the rest. That has a hole. A table present in an expiring snapshot but absent from newer ones — added then removed, renamed, or skipped by a partial/failed run — would be dropped with no copy left anywhere, even though its own most recent backup was the thing being deleted. `newestCopyPerTable(Map<suffix, tables>)` now maps each source table to the newest suffix holding a copy of it, and the purge protects exactly those, so **every table keeps its last backup independently**. Consequence worth knowing: an expired snapshot can end up partially retained (e.g. January is deleted except `legacy_table_bkp_20250101`), which is the correct trade — a coherent-looking snapshot is worth less than never destroying a table's only copy. `expiredSuffixes` was reduced to a pure date filter and no longer carries a `keepNewest` flag; protection lives entirely in the per-table check, so the two concerns are separately testable. Logging: each expired snapshot that retains anything emits one INFO line naming the count, the snapshot, the retention/cutoff and up to 8 table names (`summarise()` truncates — with ~155 tables the per-table form would bury the message), plus a closing summary line with dropped/retained/failed counts. UI hint under "Delete Backups Older Than" now states the exception explicitly, in bold, including that it is recorded in the log. `BackupRetentionTest` grew to 16 cases; the ones that matter are "a table missing from newer snapshots still keeps its own last copy" (the bug this change fixes), "the rule holds across many tables at once", and "table names containing the source of another are not confused" (`user` vs `app_user`). `./mvnw test` green at 184.

**2026-08-18: backup retention period + ALL scope now really means all tables** — two changes to the Service Admin "Database Backup Configuration".

**(1) The 43-table bug.** `BackupService.ALL_TABLES` was a hand-maintained `List.of(...)` — 2 family + 6 income + 35 others = exactly 43 names. Every table added since it was written (payroll, plaid, song book, connect, public-page-visit, subscription, login_attempt_log, …) was silently excluded, so with the schema at ~155 tables roughly **two thirds of the database was not being backed up** and nothing indicated it. Replaced with `discoverAllTables()`, reading `information_schema.tables` for `table_schema='public' AND table_type='BASE TABLE'` at run time, so a table added next month is backed up next month with no code change. Four deliberate exclusions in `EXCLUDED_TABLES`: `spring_session`/`spring_session_attributes` (live login state — stale within minutes, and restoring resurrects dead sessions) and `backup_config`/`backup_log` (restoring them would roll back the very settings being used to perform the restore). **Critically, snapshot tables are excluded by pattern** — without that, discovery finds `app_user_bkp_20260217` and snapshots it as `app_user_bkp_20260217_bkp_20260818`, doubling storage every run. `restoreSnapshot` is likewise now driven by the snapshot tables that actually exist for a date rather than a fixed list, so a restore covers exactly what the backup contained. FAMILY/INCOME remain curated subsets.

**(2) Retention.** New `backup_config.retention_months` (default 12 = 1 year, 0 = keep for ever) plus a "Delete Backups Older Than" dropdown in Backup Schedule (3/6 months, 1/2/3/5 years, never). `purgeExpiredBackups` drops snapshots strictly older than `today - retentionMonths` — worked example from the requirement: retention 6 months on 2026-08-18 gives cutoff 2026-02-18, so `app_user_bkp_20260217` is dropped and `..._20260218` is kept. Runs after each successful backup **and** daily from the scheduler even when scheduled backups are disabled (turning backups off should still let existing snapshots age out). Purge deliberately runs only after a *successful* snapshot, so a failed backup can never be the run that deletes the last good one. **The most recent snapshot is never dropped regardless of age** — retention and interval are configured independently, and e.g. a 1-month retention with a 12-month interval would otherwise leave zero backups for eleven months; the decision is logged when it applies. Only names matching `^(.+)_bkp_(\d{8})$` with a parseable calendar date are ever considered for `DROP`, re-validated immediately before the statement. Decision logic extracted as the pure static `expiredSuffixes(...)` so the rule is tested directly rather than inferred: `BackupRetentionTest` has 14 cases including the requirement's worked example, the strict boundary, calendar-correct month arithmetic (31 Aug − 6 months = 28 Feb), impossible dates left alone, and "ordinary tables are never mistaken for snapshots". `./mvnw test` green at 182. **Watch storage**: ALL scope went 43 → ~155 tables, so with a 1-month interval and 1-year retention the database would hold ~12 full copies of itself — fine at the current 12-month interval, worth checking before shortening it, especially on the Burstable tier.

**2026-08-17 (later 5): build output cleaned — the test suite was printing a fake ERROR stack trace and 252 audit lines** — a green `./mvnw test` was emitting an 80-frame `IllegalStateException: geo backend exploded` at ERROR, which reads exactly like a production failure and isn't one: `SecurityAuditServiceTest.auditFailureDoesNotPropagate` deliberately makes `GeoIpService.resolve` throw to prove a geolocation failure never costs a user their sign-in, and `SecurityAuditService` correctly catching-and-logging it is the behaviour under test. Correct behaviour, terrible ergonomics — a stack trace in a passing build teaches people to skim past ERROR, which is precisely when they stop seeing real ones. That test now attaches its own `ListAppender` to the `SecurityAuditService` logger with `additive=false`, so the trace stays off the console AND the noise becomes assertions: exactly one event, at ERROR, naming the affected account, with the cause attached. Separately, `logback-test.xml` now sets `com.churchgeniuspro.security.audit` to `OFF` for the suite — `LoginProtectionServiceTest` simulates brute-force attacks and legitimately generated ~252 `AUTH_FAILURE` lines per run; `SecurityAuditServiceTest` re-enables the channel explicitly and restores it in teardown, so no coverage is lost. Build output is now zero stack traces and zero audit lines across 168 passing tests. General rule worth keeping: a test that proves an error path works should assert on the log event, not print it.

**2026-08-17 (later 4): correction — prudent mode works fine on the /home SMB mount; Kudu's cached file sizes caused the misdiagnosis** — after fixing the `HOME=/root` bug, `/home/LogFiles/churchgeniuspro/` appeared with all three files at **0 bytes** when listed from Kudu, which looked like confirmation that logback's prudent-mode file locking fails on Azure Files. It does not. Listed from the **app container** (Development Tools -> SSH) the same files showed `application-2026-08-17.log` at 22,478 bytes — Kudu's view of file metadata on the shared mount is cached and had reported 0 for a file actively being written. On Linux App Service Kudu and the app are separate containers sharing only the `/home` mount; Kudu cannot see the app's processes or environment either, which is what hid `HOME=/root` in the first place. **Operational rule: verify log file sizes with `wc -c` from the app container, never from Kudu.** `CGP_LOG_PRUDENT` stays defaulted to `false`, but the justification is now overhead (a lock round-trip per write on a network mount, pointless on a single instance) rather than correctness — LOGGING.md §4 corrected accordingly, since it had asserted the locking was unreliable. Net result of the whole episode: exactly ONE real bug (`HOME=/root` -> audit trail on ephemeral storage), plus two wrong theories (stale JAR, prudent-mode locking) that each cost a debugging round because Kudu's container boundary and metadata caching made healthy behaviour look broken.

**2026-08-17 (later 3): THE bug — `HOME` is `/root` on Linux App Service, so the audit trail was written to scratch storage** — first production deploy created no `/home/LogFiles/churchgeniuspro/` at all. It was not prudent mode (that was a real but separate latent risk, fixed in the previous entry). `LogDirectoryPropertyDefiner` built `$HOME/LogFiles/churchgeniuspro`, on the assumption that App Service sets `HOME` to the persistent Azure Files share. It does on **Windows** App Service — which is exactly why the assumption looked sound — but on the **Linux Java SE** container the process runs as root, so `HOME=/root`, container-local storage wiped on every restart, redeploy and instance move. Diagnosis came from the app container's SSH (NOT Kudu — separate containers on Linux App Service; Kudu sees only the shared `/home` mount, which is why `find /home` came back empty while `find /` found `/root/LogFiles/churchgeniuspro/application-2026-08-17.log`). The files existed, the app was healthy, nothing errored, and every log would have evaporated at the next restart. Fix: identify the share by a property only it has — it is the directory containing `site` (as in `/home/site/wwwroot`). `HOME` is still tried first (correct on Windows) but only accepted if it passes that test; candidates then `/home`, `D:\home`. **Second-pass ordering matters and was itself a bug caught during verification**: when no marker is found, `HOME` is deliberately excluded from the fallback, because `/root` exists and is writable so including it resolved happily straight back to scratch storage. Fallback is a known mount point or nothing. Immediate unblock for a deployed-but-broken build, no rebuild needed: set `CGP_LOG_DIR=/home/LogFiles/churchgeniuspro` — the override path was always correct. `LogDestinationTest` gained 4 cases pinning this, including "HOME=/root is rejected" and "the fallback never lands on HOME". `./mvnw test` green at 168; resolution also exercised against a simulated App Service environment outside the test suite, which is what caught the fallback ordering.

**2026-08-17 (later 2): logback prudent mode disabled by default — it was silently writing nothing on App Service** — first production deploy of the logging work produced NO `/home/LogFiles/churchgeniuspro/` directory at all, despite the JAR containing `logback-spring.xml` and the app starting normally. Root cause: `<prudent>true</prudent>` was hardcoded on all three file appenders. Prudent mode takes an exclusive byte-range lock per write so several JVMs can share one file — correct in theory for a scaled-out App Service, but the `/home` share is an Azure Files (SMB) mount where that locking is not dependable, and when it fails **logback writes nothing and reports nothing**: no exception, no failed request, no log directory. An unusually quiet way to lose an audit trail. Now `<prudent>${CGP_LOG_PRUDENT:-false}</prudent>` — off by default, since it only ever helps when scaled out and this app runs a single instance. Scaling out is handled instead by `CGP_LOG_PER_INSTANCE_FILES=true`, which gives each instance its own `security-2026-08-17-<instance>.log` and needs no locking at all; the retention sweeper already recognised that shape. `CGP_LOG_PRUDENT=true` remains available. Also added `LogRetentionService.reportLogDestination()` (`@PostConstruct`): one startup line reporting the resolved log directory, whether it exists and is writable, and the env vars the resolution depends on (`HOME`, `WEBSITE_INSTANCE_ID`, `CGP_LOG_DIR`, `CGP_LOG_PRUDENT`, `CGP_LOG_PER_INSTANCE_FILES`) — logged at WARN when unusable so it surfaces despite `logging.level.root=WARN`. That line turns this class of failure from a 30-minute Kudu investigation into a glance at the log stream. LOGGING.md §4 rewritten with the new default plus a troubleshooting table. Verified: `./mvnw test` green at 164, and all three file-writing modes (default / prudent / per-instance) exercised against the real logback config outside the test suite. NOTE for future debugging: on Linux App Service the Kudu debug console and the app run in SEPARATE containers — Kudu cannot see the app's processes or environment, only the shared `/home` mount. Use Development Tools -> SSH for the app container.

**2026-08-17 (later): Azure App Service X-Forwarded-For port bug — brute-force protection was defeated in production** — App Service's front end sends `X-Forwarded-For: <client-ip>:<port>`, i.e. the client IP **with the ephemeral source port attached** (unusual; most proxies send a bare address). `ClientIpResolver.resolve` took the first hop verbatim, so every request from one client produced a different rate-limit key: the username+IP and IP scopes would never have reached a second failure, and the whole failed-login protection would have been silently inert on production with no error logged anywhere. Geolocation would also have failed to parse every address, and audit lines would have carried `ip=203.0.113.7:54321`. New `ClientIpResolver.stripPort` handles IPv4 (`203.0.113.7:54321`) and bracketed IPv6 (`[2001:db8::1]:54321`), and deliberately leaves bare IPv6 (`2001:db8::1`) and IPv4-mapped IPv6 (`::ffff:203.0.113.7`) untouched; a malformed value is passed through rather than truncated into something that looks valid. **The same bug existed in `ForgotUsernameController.clientIp()`** (pre-existing, unrelated to this work) — its documented "5 recovery attempts per hour per IP" limit had therefore never worked on Azure; it now delegates to the shared resolver. New `ClientIpResolverTest` (14 tests) pins it, including the assertion that three different ephemeral ports from one client collapse to one rate-limit key. Confirmed environment: resource group `churchgeniuspro_group`, plan `ASP-churchgeniusprogroup-a30f` **Premium v3 P0v3**, Central US, **Java SE / Java 25** (embedded server, no Tomcat container) — `trust-forwarded-headers=true` is correct for this host and must stay on. LOGGING.md §2 rewritten with the real environment, §2.5 added covering this quirk plus the **Always On** requirement (without it App Service unloads the app and NO `@Scheduled` job runs — log sweep, retention purge, and the nine existing reminder crons all stop). Also documented: daily files roll at UTC midnight = 7pm Central (`WEBSITE_TIME_ZONE` would fix it but shifts the existing reminder crons, so left as a deliberate decision), and deployment slots keep their own `/home`. `./mvnw test` green at 164 tests.

**2026-08-17: Daily categorised logging + login/logout audit trail (Azure App Service)** — full write-up in **`LOGGING.md`**. New `logback-spring.xml` replaces Spring Boot's default file appender with three daily files, rolled at midnight, 30-day retention: `security-YYYY-MM-DD.log` (LOGIN/LOGOUT), `error-YYYY-MM-DD.log` (ERROR + stack traces), `application-YYYY-MM-DD.log`. **Durability on App Service**: `LogDirectoryPropertyDefiner` resolves the directory at startup — `CGP_LOG_DIR` override, else (detected via `WEBSITE_INSTANCE_ID`) `$HOME/LogFiles/churchgeniuspro`, else `./logs`. `$HOME` is the persistent Azure Files share and is the only writable path that survives restarts, redeployments and instance moves — and it resolves correctly on Linux (`/home`) *and* Windows (`D:\home`) App Service without knowing which we run. Multi-instance safe via logback `prudent=true` (exclusive lock per write, keeps the exact filenames); `CGP_LOG_PER_INSTANCE_FILES=true` switches to `security-2026-08-17-<instance>.log` if lock contention ever matters. **The database is the store of record for security events**, not the files: every LOGIN/LOGOUT also writes to `login_attempt_log`, which survives things the share does not. New `SecurityAuditService` emits `LOGIN | church=… | username=… | role=… | at=… | ip=… | city=… | state=… | country=… | sessionRef=…` to a dedicated `com.churchgeniuspro.security.audit` logger (`additivity=false`); audits the remember-me auto-login too, and never lets an audit failure cost a user their sign-in. Geolocation via **MaxMind GeoLite2** read from a local file (`geoip2:4.4.0`, `scripts/update-geoip.sh`, default `$HOME/data/GeoLite2-City.mmdb`) — no outbound call on the login path, no member IP sent off-site; every failure mode (missing/corrupt file, bad address, not found) degrades to `city=- state=- country=-` and the app starts fine without it. **Credentials never reach disk**: `MaskingPatternLayout` scrubs the *rendered* line (stack traces included — exception messages are the common leak) on every appender, covering passwords/tokens/secrets/API keys/`Authorization`/`Cookie`/BCrypt hashes/vendor key shapes across JSON, `key=value`, query strings and SCREAMING_SNAKE env names. Two masker bugs the tests caught: `\b` does not fire between `_` and a letter so `\bENC_KEY\b` missed `PLAID_TOKEN_ENC_KEY` (now `(?<![A-Za-z0-9])` guards), and shape patterns must run unconditionally rather than behind the trigger-word fast path. Session ids are now **hashed everywhere** — `ClientIpResolver.sessionHash()` replaces `sessionId()` (no accessor for the raw value remains), `login_attempt_log.session_id` is **dropped** in favour of `session_hash`, and the audit field is named `sessionRef` so the correlation hash is not itself redacted. Retention enforced twice: logback `maxHistory=30` + `cleanHistoryOnStart`, and `LogRetentionService` (03:40 daily) sweeping orphaned files logback no longer tracks — narrowly scoped to this app's own filename shapes, refuses to run below 1 day, and asserted not to touch `GeoLite2-City.mmdb` or anything else. `GlobalExceptionHandler` now logs user/church/role/IP plus a `ref=` short id also returned in the 500 body. `logging.file.name`/`logging.logback.rollingpolicy.*` removed (inert once a custom logback config exists). Verified: `./mvnw test` green (150 tests, 44 new) **and** an out-of-suite run of the real logback config, which is what caught `%wEx` — Spring Boot's throwable converter, unregistered for a standalone config, which would have printed `%PARSER_ERROR[wEx]` on every production line (now `%ex`). NOTE: the repo has no Azure deployment config (`scripts/deploy.sh` is a no-op), so OS/plan/instance count could not be read — LOGGING.md §2.4 has the `az` commands to confirm, and §2.3 the optional Diagnostic Setting for off-box archive. Remaining recommendations in LOGGING.md §10.

**2026-08-16: Failed-login protection (brute-force / credential-stuffing)** — new `service/LoginProtectionService` is now the single decision point for every authentication entry point; full write-up in **`LOGIN_SECURITY.md`**. **No account is ever permanently locked** — the deliberate rejection of "lock after 5 failures", which would let anyone who knows a username (they are public: invites, directory, approval links) lock that person out at will. Instead: append-only `login_attempt_log` (audit + counters) and `login_block` (temporary blocks, each with a hard `blocked_until`), both in Postgres so the limits hold across instances — no in-memory state, no session affinity. Three scopes, all counted from the same rolling window: **USER_IP** (username+IP, 5/15min → 15m block, ×2 escalation to max 2h) is the primary control and means an attacker hammering `john@…` blocks only *their own* pair while John signs in normally; **IP** (20/15min) catches username spraying; **USERNAME** (20/15min, fixed 15m, never escalates, `account-scope-enabled=false` to disable) is the distributed-attack backstop and the only scope a third party can trip for someone else — kept short, un-ratchetable (attempts made *during* a block are audited but not counted), and released the moment the owner completes a password reset. Below the threshold: progressive delay 400ms/800ms/… capped at 2s. Successful login resets the account-scoped counters but **not** the IP counter (else one valid account on a host wipes the IP budget between sprays). `LoginController`: guard runs *before* the account lookup; unknown-username now burns a dummy BCrypt compare so response time doesn't enumerate accounts; unknown-user and wrong-password responses are byte-for-byte identical; **removed the submitted/stored password *length* from the 401 log line**; correct-password-but-policy-refused (inactive/disabled/lapsed) is audited as DENIED and never counted. 429 + `Retry-After` on block, with a message naming neither threshold nor attempts remaining. `LoginThrottleFilter` (order 0) applies IP-scope protection to `/api/serviceadmin/login`, `/api/temp-access/login`, `/api/ntag-login/*`. Password reset hardened: 256-bit `SecureRandom` tokens (was `UUID`), new `password_reset_token.created_at` + 2-minute per-account email cooldown (inbox-flood protection that cannot lock anyone out — the earlier link stays valid), and a completed reset clears the account counters + releases its blocks (the self-service escape hatch that makes the account scope safe). All thresholds/durations under `security.login-protection.*`; `trust-forwarded-headers=true` by default — **verify against the actual proxy setup**, see LOGIN_SECURITY.md §8. 7 new indexes in `DatabaseIndexInitializer`; `migrate_production.sql` updated (2 tables, 2 sequences, 7 indexes, `created_at` backfill). Nightly retention purge at 03:20, 30-day default. `signup.locked` deliberately untouched. Verified: full `./mvnw test` green (105 tests, 24 new); JPQL executed against a real generated schema. Remaining recommendations (no CAPTCHA, no MFA, plaintext reset tokens, `localStorage` password in login.html, `System.out` email logging in ForgotPasswordController) are listed in LOGIN_SECURITY.md §9.

**2026-07-25: Event reminder email fixes (same-day + N-day)** — 4 bugs in `ReminderSchedulerService` event reminders (`/event?id=6` "4 &amp; 14" report). (1) **Invented end time**: `buildIcsContent` fabricated DTEND=start+1h when no end time configured — Gmail's ICS preview card then showed "6:00 PM – 7:00 PM". Now DTEND is OMITTED for timed events without an end (RFC 5545: event ends at DTSTART); all-day DTEND=next-day kept; same fix in `buildCalendarLinksHtml` Google/Outlook deeplinks + `eventGoogleCalendarUrl` ({calendar} placeholder) — end mirrors start instead of +1h. (2) **Raw state code in location**: ICS LOCATION + calendar-link `location=` params were hand-built with `event.getState()` raw ("Olathe, 16, USA") — both now reuse `buildFullAddress` (States.getCode conversion → "KS"; ICS appends country). (3) **`&amp;` shown literally**: event names stored HTML-escaped rendered wrong in subjects/SMS/push (no unescape) and DOUBLE-escaped in bodies (`esc()` at call site + `wrapInTemplate` esc()'s the heading again). New `decodeEntities()` (single-pass, `&amp;` last — raw text never corrupted) + `eventDisplayName(event)` used in all 3 subjects, push titles, SMS, ICS SUMMARY/DESCRIPTION, link text/details; body/template headings now passed PLAIN to `wrapInTemplate` (it escapes itself) — fixes pre-existing double-escape for ALL event names containing `&`. (4) **Get Directions overflow on mobile**: the 3-button row was a fixed `<table>` (Google|Outlook|Directions) that overflowed the rounded box on phones — replaced with wrapping `display:inline-block` anchors (shared btnStyle, `margin:0 8px 8px 0`). Applies to same-day AND before-days (3-day) reminders — both share these builders. Verified via javac parse + extracted-method harness (24 assertions, incl. DTEND omitted/kept, KS conversion, single-escape, no-table wrap). Restart needed.

**2026-07-24: Public Event Calendar — Events & Meetings only + details dialog** — the Public Screens "Event Calendar" link (`/viewEventCalendar?cid=…`) now shows ONLY Church Events and Meetings; member birthdays/anniversaries are filtered SERVER-SIDE. `EventCalendarController`: `GET /api/event-calendar/public-events` filters via new `isPublicType()` (meeting|churchevent); `buildIcsFeed` gained `includePersonal` flag — authenticated `/api/event-calendar/ics` passes true (unchanged), public `/api/event-calendar/public-ics` passes false (no birthdays/anniversaries in the public Google/iPhone sync feed). Shared `buildEvents` entries enriched (additive — internal eventcalendar.html unaffected): meetings carry `location` (new `joinAddress` helper, numeric state codes → abbreviations via `common/States`) + `description` (note); church events (new `churchEventEntry` helper) carry `eventId`, `location`, `description` (note), `organizer` (hostName), `fee`, `registrationLink`; public+auth ICS church-event VEVENTs now emit LOCATION. `viewEventCalendar.html`: legend reduced to Event/Meeting, birthday/anniversary CSS removed, defensive `publicOnly()` client filter on all fetches, detail-panel rows now clickable (chevron, keyboard-accessible) → new view-only details dialog `evtOverlay` (type badge/tag, long date, time or "All day", location + Google Maps "Get Directions" link, organizer, fee, Recurring flag, pre-wrap description, "Register for this Event" button when registrationLink is http(s); ESC/backdrop/✕ close). No auth changes needed — `/api/event-calendar/public-` already whitelisted in AuthFilter; Public Screens already lists Event Calendar. Verified via javac parse + jsdom test (filtering, modal fields, esc-injection, register button). Updated file also copied into `target/classes/static/`; run `./mvnw compile` + restart for the controller change.

Connect tab lists `/api/connect-admin` (status filter + search); Prayer tab lists `/api/ppr` (status filter + search); both use a shared `mgOverlay` Manage modal — detail grid, assign (connect → `/api/followups/assignees` + `/api/connect-admin/{id}/assign` with memberId+name; prayer → `/api/ppr/volunteers` + `/api/ppr/{id}/assign`), status select (`…/status` on each), history (connect → `/api/followups/linked?linkedType=CONNECT` + "+ Add Follow-Up" via POST /api/followups; prayer → `/api/ppr/{id}/notes` + "+ Add Note" via `/note`). Lazy-loaded per tab; counts in tab labels. NO backend changes — all endpoints already existed. `donation.html` (public /donate/{token}): re-skinned to the public design system (`--brand:#673147`, bg `#f3eef1`, solid-brand hero with round 72px logo, borders `#e0d5db`/`#eadfe5`, radius 9/11/16, brand focus ring `rgba(103,49,71,.1)`, submit hover darkens to `#572a3d` — removed all `#3949ab` blue gradient remnants; thank-you title brand-colored). **2026-07-22 (later 4): Upcoming Events notes render rich HTML** — `static/upcoming-events.html` showed raw editor HTML (`<p>`, `<em>`…) because notes were esc()'d. New client-side allowlist sanitizer (`richHtml`/`sanitizeNode`): OK tags p/br/em/i/strong/b/u/s/span/div/ul/ol/li/blockquote/h1-4/a; DROP_TAGS (script/style/iframe/object/embed/…) removed INCLUDING content; unknown tags unwrapped keeping text; styles filtered to text-align/font-style/font-weight/text-decoration; `<a>` only http(s) + forced target=_blank rel=noopener. `descHtml()` renders `.desc.rich` (normal whitespace + paragraph/list/blockquote spacing) when the text contains tags, else the old esc + pre-wrap for plain notes. Verified via jsdom (formatting preserved; onclick/color/img/iframe/javascript: links/script content all stripped). App restart needed.

Endpoints on SongBookController (`/api/songbook/ads` GET/POST, `/ads/{id}` PUT/DELETE, `/ads/{id}/image`, `/ads/{id}/replace`, `/ads/reorder` — needEdit for mutations, book-scoped via `?bookId=`) + public `GET /api/public/songbook/ad-image?t=&id=`. **2026-07-22: Connect submissions admin + auto-response + public-form spam protection** — public Connect With Us submissions now have an admin-facing record: new `hibernate/ConnectSubmission` (`connect_submission`, DDL in migrate_production.sql; status New|Assigned|InProgress|Contacted|Closed, assigned_member_id/assigned_to, member_id link to the created Visitor, follow_up_id, confirmation_sent) + repo. `PublicEngagementService.connect()` now also saves the submission, links the auto FollowUp as `linkedType="CONNECT"`/`linkedId=submissionId` (was VISITOR/memberId), and sends the confirmation (`CONFIRMATION_MSG` "Thank you for contacting us…") via `sendOrgEmail` when email present + SMS via new `WhatsAppSenderService.sendSingleSms(phone,body,clientId)` (public quota-checked single-number wrapper) when phone present AND prefs include Text; `prayer()` sends a confirmation email too. Admin: `GET /connectAdmin` page (`connect-admin.html` — status-filter tabs, Manage modal with detail grid, assign dropdown fed by `/api/followups/assignees` writing memberId+name, status select, linked follow-up history via `/api/followups/linked?linkedType=CONNECT` + inline "+ Add Follow-Up" posting to `/api/followups`) + APIs `GET /api/connect-admin`, `POST /api/connect-admin/{id}/assign|/status`, `DELETE /api/connect-admin/{id}` (guard requireAdminOrUser + clientId, same as /api/ppr); shell.js nav entry admin/connect-submissions. **Spam protection** (Connect AND publicPrayer): new `util/PublicFormGuard` — honeypot field `website` (fake success), time-trap `formToken` issued in `/api/public/engage-info` (encrypted `PFT|cid|ts`, min 3s / max 2h age), per-IP sliding-window rate limit (5/10min, 15/h per form, X-Forwarded-For aware), server-side email/phone/length/link-spam validation, and OPTIONAL Google reCAPTCHA (auto-enabled when `public.forms.recaptcha.secret`(+`.site-key`) configured — engage-info then returns `captchaSiteKey`, pages render the widget, server verifies; fail-open on network errors). `spamCheck()` runs in `PublicEngagementController` before both public submits; both forms carry hidden honeypot + formToken (+captchaToken). Success responses include `message` = confirmation text shown on the thank-you panel. Tests: `PublicFormGuardTest` (8 tests). Sandbox has no JDK 25 — run `./mvnw test -Dtest=PublicFormGuardTest` + `./mvnw compile` locally.

**2026-07-21: Delete occurrences of recurring meetings (/meetings + Event Calendar)** — recurring meetings stay ONE `meeting` row; deleting a single occurrence records an exception in new `hibernate/MeetingSkipDate` (`meeting_skip_date`, unique meeting_id+skip_date, DDL in migrate_production.sql) + `MeetingSkipDateRepository`. New `util/MeetingRecurrence` (shared `occursOn`/`listOccurrences` engine mirroring calendar+scheduler semantics) + `service/MeetingOccurrenceService` (tenant-checked listOccurrences/deleteOccurrences/restoreOccurrences/deleteFromDate — delete-future sets `endDate=fromDate-1`, or soft-deletes the series when nothing remains, and purges out-of-range skips). Endpoints on MeetingController (guard `general.meetings.delete`): `GET /api/meetings/{id}/occurrences?limit`, `POST …/occurrences/delete {dates[]}`, `…/restore {dates[]}`, `…/delete-future {fromDate}`. Expansion points now exclude skips: `EventCalendarController.buildEvents` (bulk skip map; meeting events now carry `meetingId`+`recurring`), ICS feed emits `EXDATE` lines (TZID or VALUE=DATE matching DTSTART), and `ReminderSchedulerService` — type-1 half-hourly meeting reminder checks `isOccurrenceSkipped(m, today)` (suppresses email/SMS/WhatsApp/push), weekly digest filters via new `firstActiveOccurrenceInWeek` (also used as the effective date for per-meeting WhatsApp lines). UI: `meeting.html` — 📆 Manage Occurrences button on recurring rows → `occModal` (next 26 occurrences, per-row 🗑 delete / ↩️ restore of skipped, checkbox multi-select "Delete Selected", "Delete This & All Future…" prompt); `eventcalendar.html` — detail panel meeting items show 🗑 (this occurrence; whole meeting for one-time) + ⏭🗑 (this & future) gated by CGP_PERMS, refreshing month/week after change. Tests: `MeetingOccurrenceServiceTest` (12 tests — recurrence engine incl. nth-weekday, skip listing, single/multi delete, dedupe/no-op rejection, tenant check, restore, delete-future both branches). Sandbox has no JDK 25 — run `./mvnw test -Dtest=MeetingOccurrenceServiceTest` + `./mvnw compile` locally to confirm.

**2026-07-16 (later 10): AI Search natural-language DATA answers (permission-aware)** — new `service/AiDataSearchService` (deterministic, NO LLM call — works without OpenAI) + `controller/AiDataSearchController` (`POST /api/ai-search/data` {query} → {handled, answer, denied, intent}; fail-open handled:false on errors). Intents: volunteers list (VolunteerProfile + member names/skills), person giving ("Did Anson give tithe this month?" — member-matched income rows, tithe filters on fund name), income summary ("What was the income for March?" — new `IncomeRepository.findRangeByAppClientId` with JOIN FETCH member+subSource because open-in-view=false), birthdays/anniversaries (person lookup "When is Anson's birthday?" via longest-name match with possessive stripping, or period lists using birthday/anniversary month+day fields). `parsePeriod` handles today/yesterday/tomorrow, this/last week, this/last/next month, this/last year, month names ± year, bare 20xx years, and (next) weekday names. **Permissions mirror the page routes** (RoleGuard requirePermission/requireMemberPermission semantics): members data = Admin/Accountant/User staff + admin.family, or member portal + member.directory; finance = SuperAdmin/Admin/Accountant + reports.income (member portal DENIED); volunteers = SuperAdmin/Admin/User + general.volunteers (member portal DENIED); church sessions bypass. Denials return `AiDataSearchService.DENIED_MSG` and no data. Frontend `static/ai-assistant.js`: `runAssist` split — after directNav, `looksLikeDataQuestion()` regex gates a `tryDataSearch()` fetch; handled → renderCard (source "Church Data", spoken in voice mode, logged in converse); else falls into `assistFlow(query, input)` (the original converse/local/delegate/LLM flow, unchanged). Works in Type/Voice/Converse (all route text through runAssist). Tests: `AiDataSearchServiceTest` (13 tests — intents, date parsing, member-portal + revoked-perm denials) — all pass via sandbox JUnit/Mockito.

TRIAL option added to serviceadminhome Subscription Type dropdown (Active Period optional for Trial).

**2026-07-14: Subscription Plans (Phase 1 foundation)** — per-client plans with limits + feature flags. New: `hibernate/SubscriptionPlan` (limits null=unlimited; `features_json` map where missing key=enabled) + `SubscriptionUsage` (monthly email/SMS/giving counters, unique client+month) + repos; `SubscriptionPlanSeeder` seeds FREE/STANDARD/PRO idempotently; `service/SubscriptionService` (plan resolution via `ServiceClient.subscriptionType` FREE→FREE/LIMITED→STANDARD/FULL→PRO or direct plan_code, 60s cache, fail-open); `util/SubscriptionFeatureCatalog` (28 feature keys → URL prefixes, longest-prefix match); `webfilter/SubscriptionFeatureFilter` (order 6, 403 JSON for /api/*, HTML notice for pages); `GET /api/subscription/features` (frontend payload); Service Admin CRUD `GET/POST/PUT /api/serviceadmin/subscription-plans` + 💳 Plans card UI on serviceadminhome. Extra SMS credits are PER CLIENT (`ServiceClient.extraSmsCount`, editable in the Registered Clients form; effective SMS limit = plan limit + client extra), NOT on the plan. Enforcement hooks: email quota in `EmailService.sendOrgEmail`; SMS quota via `WhatsAppSenderService.smsWithQuota` choke-point; giving quota gated at `DonationController` intent + recorded at save; people limit in `FamilyController POST /api/family`; member-portal limit+feature in `MemberSignupController.verify` (`LoginRepository.countByChurchIdAndChurchFalseAndDeletedFalse`); kids-portal limit in `KidsMinistryController` child create/batch (`FamilyMemberRepository.countChildRoleMembers`); publicScreens page options filtered server-side. Frontend: `session.js` fetches features → `window.CGP_FEATURES`/`CGP_hasFeature` + `FEATURE_NAV` nav hiding + `[data-feature]` element gate; events.html Check-In button + kidsMinistry Check-In tab gated. DDL in `migrate_production.sql`. NOTE: sandbox compile verified everything except FamilyController/MemberSignupController (mount truncation; edits reviewed) — run `./mvnw compile` to confirm.

**2026-07-14: Password policy + Service Admin change password** — new `util/PasswordPolicy` (8+ chars, upper, lower, digit, special; `validate()` returns per-rule failure message) enforced server-side in `SignupController` (send-code + verify), `MemberSignupController` (send-code + verify), `ChurchRegistrationController`, `ForgotPasswordController` (was an inconsistent 6-char check), and the new `POST /api/serviceadmin/change-password` (self-authenticating: verifies current password; "Change Password" toggle form on `serviceAdminLogin.html`). Client-side live requirements checklist (✓/✕ per rule) added to `signup.html`, `memberSignup.html`, `churchregistration.html`, `resetPassword.html`, `serviceAdminLogin.html`. Also fixed latent plaintext-password write in `MembershipFormController` (now BCrypt via `PasswordUtil.encode`).

**Newest (2026-07-08/09): Public marketing website (/web/*)** — five anonymous, responsive marketing pages in `static/web/` (home, features, pricing, compare, help) sharing `static/web/site.css`; served via `controller/PublicWebController` (`GET /web/{page:[a-zA-Z]+}` records a `hibernate/PublicPageVisit` row then forwards to the static file — the regex constraint prevents an infinite forward loop with `*.html`). Help & Support were merged into `/web/help` (docs + FAQ + contact form) and the Info page was removed; legacy `/web/support` (topic param preserved) and `/web/info` redirect. Public contact form `POST /api/web/contact` (whitelisted via `"/api/web"` in `AuthFilter.PUBLIC_PREFIXES`) emails info@churchgeniuspro.com with a honeypot spam guard. Visitor stats: `GET /api/serviceadmin/public-page-stats` (guarded by the `serviceAdminId` session attribute) feeding a "🌐 Public Website Visitors" card on `serviceadminhome.html`. DDL in `migrate_production.sql` (`public_page_visit`). NOTE: no self-serve signup exists (onboarding is invitation-based), so "Sign Up for Free" buttons lead to `/web/help?topic=signup` which prefills the contact form as lead capture. Also: spring-boot-devtools (optional/runtime) + local-profile `spring.web.resources.static-locations` file-serving for live UI reload; mail password moved to `${MAIL_PASSWORD:}` env var.

**Latest (2026-07-08): Event Invitations ("Send Invite")** — the reminder contact list doubles as an invitation list. `EventRegistrationReminderService.sendInvitations` sends "You're Invited" email (church-name header, RSVP Now button) and/or SMS to each valid contact; per-recipient dedupe via `event_registration_reminder_log.message_type` (`INVITE` vs `REMINDER` — new column, mirrored in `migrate_production.sql`) so invites and reminders never block each other; re-sending only reaches new contacts. `POST /api/events/{id}/registration-reminder/invite`; 📨 Send Invite button in the events.html modal (enabled only with ≥1 valid contact, save-then-send, sent/skipped summary, Type column in attempt log). Earlier same day: RSVP wording pass on reminder email/SMS, manual `…/run?force=true` endpoint + 🚀 Send Now button, scheduler observability logging, `spring.task.scheduling.pool.size=4`, `/bankSync` shell.css + base-style fix. Tests: 25 in `EventRegistrationReminderServiceTest`.

**Just completed (2026-07-08): Event Registration Reminder feature** — per-event optional list of email/phone contacts who get a "please register" email/SMS N days before the event, only if not already registered for that event (phones compared normalized to 10 digits). New: `hibernate/EventRegistrationReminderContact`, `EventRegistrationReminderLog` (+ repos), `service/EventRegistrationReminderService` (own `@Scheduled` hourly cron `scheduler.job.registration-reminders`; once-per-schedule gate via `reminder_sent_log` type `EVENT_REG_REMINDER`; per-recipient dedupe via SENT log rows; all attempts logged with reason), `controller/EventRegistrationReminderController` (`GET/PUT /api/events/{id}/registration-reminder`, `GET …/logs`), 2 new columns on `ChurchEvent` (`registration_reminder_enabled/days`), modal UI in `static/events.html` (🔔 Reg Reminder button per card, incl. collapsible attempt-log table), DDL mirrored in `migrate_production.sql`. Unit tests: `service/EventRegistrationReminderServiceTest` (17 tests — normalization, send rules incl. the spec's worked example, dedupe, gating; pure Mockito, no Spring context).
